And when my friend found me, I was saying out loud that I couldn’t keep living like this.\n\nDoctor: Before this current episode, was there ever another time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I can’t remember another clear time like this before. It started pretty soon after my divorce. It’s been going on since then… about a year and eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Since this began after your divorce, has there been any period of at least two months when you felt mostly back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, not really. And honestly, my friend has been checking on me because I’m not reliably eating or showering, and I don’t feel like I can always keep myself safe.\n\nDoctor: Did this start soon after a major stressful event or big life change?\n\nPatient: Yes. It started pretty soon after my divorce. It’s been going on since then… about a year and eleven months now.\n\nDoctor: Since this began after your divorce, has there been any period of at least two months when you felt mostly back to your usual self?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Since shortly after the divorce it’s just kind of stayed with me. It’s been about a year and eleven months now, and I don’t think there’s been a two-month stretch where I felt like my usual self.\n\nDoctor: Before your divorce, was there ever a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things nearly every day for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I had rough times before, but I can’t clearly say there was a two-week period like that before the divorce.\n\nDoctor: For the past two years, have you felt depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up feeling really low… like there’s this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It feels like there isn’t much of a future for me beyond my apartment.\n\nDoctor: Do your feelings since the divorce seem much stronger than you would expect for what happened?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does feel stronger. Most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest, and it’s like I can’t see any real future for myself beyond my apartment walls.\n\nDoctor: Have other people noticed that you’ve been moving or speaking much more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes. Some days I can’t sit still and I pace from the kitchen to the window over and over until my legs hurt. Other times I’m the opposite… I move so slowly that even making coffee feels like I’m lifting wet cement.\n\nDoctor: When you feel at your lowest, have you heard voices or become convinced of things that other people say aren’t true?\n\nPatient: Yes. A lot of this past year, when I’ve been really low, the voices are there too. I’ll be lying in bed feeling hopeless, and I hear this woman saying I ruined everything. It feels very real when it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard that woman’s voice at a time when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yes. There was a stretch before the depression got really bad where I wasn’t feeling as low yet. I could still cook sometimes, and I’d answer friends. But I was already hearing that woman’s voice, and for weeks I was also thinking the hallway lights were signals about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt a little better than usual, but not unusually energetic or euphoric like that.\n\nDoctor: Before this current period, have you ever had another time in your life when you felt depressed or lost interest in things for a couple of weeks or longer?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure about another time before this.
